\caption{Distribution of maximum-class confidence for all 8.47~million
 galaxies. The distribution is strongly bimodal, with a sharp
 high-confidence peak near unity and a secondary peak near
 $0.5$--$0.6$ corresponding to ambiguous morphologies (face-on
 ellipticals misclassifiable as smooth spirals, mergers, and
 low-surface-brightness objects). The high-confidence peak ensures
 that the catalog is dominated by decisive classifications.
 The canonical confidence stratification used by all parity analyses
 in this paper (Sec.~\ref{sec:confidence}) is the
 high/mid/low ladder defined by max-confidence thresholds
 $\{>0.9,\, 0.6\text{--}0.9,\, 0.5\text{--}0.6\}$; T7 of the bias
 audit (Table~\ref{tab:bias_tests}) reports the qualitative
 fraction-at-$>\!0.9$-confidence statistic (37.9\%) used for that
 test only. Earlier drafts also quoted a fraction-at-$>\!0.99$
 number from a different catalog snapshot; that figure is superseded
 by the canonical T7 statistic and the high-confidence stratum
 defined in Sec.~\ref{sec:confidence}.
 \label{fig:confidence_dist}}
\end{figure*}

\subsection{Global CW Fraction}
\label{sec:cw_frac}

Table~\ref{tab:cw_frac} summarizes the global CW fraction across the
three catalog tiers, with $1\sigmaunit$ binomial uncertainties
$\sigma = \sqrt{p(1-p)/N_{\rm spiral}} \approx 0.000279$ (reported as
$0.0003$ in the table after rounding to one significant figure)
for the canonical $N_{\rm spiral} = 3{,}201{,}160$
(equivariant; the older $3{,}321{,}795$ snapshot gave
$\sigma \approx 0.000274$ and is superseded). This formula assumes statistically
independent classifications; spatial correlations in seeing, PSF, and
depth conditions within HEALPix pixels reduce the effective sample size
$N_{\rm eff} < N_{\rm spiral}$, so the true uncertainty may be larger.
The spatially uniform distribution (across all 7 equatorial coordinate slabs) of the $9.5\sigmaunit$ residual
(Table~\ref{tab:sky_balance}) suggests the dominant effect is a global
training-label bias rather than spatially correlated systematics, but
a rigorous $N_{\rm eff}$ estimate (e.g., via pixel-to-pixel variance
of the CW fraction) is needed to confirm the formal significance. The raw Catalog~A shows a 0.79\%
CW excess ($\CW/(\CW+\CCW) = 0.5079 \pm 0.0003$, a $28.8\sigmaunit$
deviation from exact parity). Platt calibration (Catalog~B) reduces
this to $\sim\!0.4\%$. Equivariant averaging (Catalog~C) yields
$\CW/(\CW+\CCW) = 0.4974 \pm 0.0003$, a $0.26\%$ deficit
corresponding to $9.5\sigmaunit$ from 0.5000 (using the unrounded
canonical-$N$ $\sigma = 0.000279$: $(0.5000-0.49735)/0.000279 = 9.47\sigma$,
rounded to $9.5\sigma$).
A non-parametric bootstrap of the Catalog~A asymmetry $A \equiv (\CW-\CCW)/(\CW+\CCW)$ over $10^5$ resamples confirms the analytic Poisson estimate: $A_{\rm obs}=0.01576$ with bootstrap mean $\bar A=0.01578$, $\sigma_{\rm boot}=5.47\times 10^{-4}$, $95\%$ CI $[0.01471,\,0.01685]$, and $99\%$ CI $[0.01436,\,0.01718]$, yielding a bootstrap-derived asymmetry significance of $28.80\sigma$ in agreement with the analytic Poisson value (companion artifact deposited with the public release; see Sec.~\ref{sec:data_availability}). The bootstrap CI excludes zero at $\gg 99.9\%$ confidence and provides a self-consistency check on the raw-catalog asymmetry baseline (the $28.80\sigma$ figure is the bootstrap-stability metric of the chirality-fraction estimator, not an external-validation $\sigma$; an independent non-Anthropic cross-vendor R-round is required for that).

While formally significant, this $9.5\sigmaunit$ residual in Catalog~C
is a factor of~3 smaller than the raw Catalog~A deviation and, at
$0.26\%$ amplitude, is well below the minimum detectable
\emph{dipole} of $0.29\%$ statistical Fisher floor / $\geq 0.75\%$ empirical $50\%$-recovery-$3\sigma$ (Sec.~\ref{sec:sensitivity}).
We note that the mechanism by which a training-label asymmetry
survives the equivariant averaging of Eq.~(\ref{eq:tta}) is not
fully understood. Three candidate mechanisms are: (1)~training-set
label imbalance inherited from Galaxy Zoo~1 crowdsourcing (GZ1 has a
known ${\sim}1\%$ CW excess from human handedness bias~\cite{Land:2008}),
which propagates as a ${\sim}0.5\%$ offset in the equivariant catalog
after TTA correction; (2)~residual orientation-dependent bias not fully
corrected by 2-fold TTA (rotations beyond the horizontal flip, which
are excluded for semantic reasons discussed in Sec.~\ref{sec:tta}); and
(3)~photometric asymmetry in the DESI Legacy Surveys imaging (PSF
elongation correlates with scan direction, introducing a sub-percent
orientation-dependent signal). Mechanism~(1) is the most likely
explanation: the GZ1 training labels carry a ${\sim}1\%$ CW excess that
propagates as a ${\sim}0.5\%$ offset, consistent with the observed
$0.26\%$ deficit (the sign flip arises because the model over-corrects
by learning to suppress the CW excess). Definitive identification
requires the diagnostic $P_{\rm NS}^{\rm orig} - P_{\rm NS}^{\rm flip}$
decomposition with per-object flip-pair outputs, which the present
catalog does not provide. Until the mechanism is identified, the $0.29\%$ statistical
sensitivity claim should be interpreted as an upper bound on any
physical isotropy-breaking axial-vector \emph{dipole} signal in the parity-even ($\ell\!=\!1$) channel, not as a measurement
precision: the $9.5\sigma$ monopole offset demonstrates that sub-percent
biases can survive the equivariant procedure, and only the spatial
uniformity of the offset (Table~\ref{tab:sky_balance}) prevents it from
mimicking a physical dipole. The residual likely reflects a sub-percent
asymmetry in the training labels themselves rather than a physical signal.
Supporting this interpretation, the CE-ResNet benchmark-overlap subset
yields an equivariant CW fraction of $0.5012 \pm 0.0006$---a $0.38\%$
offset from the full-catalog value of $0.4974 \pm 0.0003$---which
brackets the sub-percent systematic floor of the equivariant
procedure.\footnote{The $0.38\%$ subset-to-full-catalog offset on the
overlap-subset value $0.5012$ is within the tier-to-tier variation
($0.79\% \to 0.4\% \to -0.26\%$) shown above.}
Crucially, this monopole offset is uniform across 7 equatorial coordinate slabs
(Table~\ref{tab:sky_balance}: all regions within $0.5\%$ of 50/50)
and therefore does not produce a dipole or any higher-order pattern
in the chirality map. This progression---from $28.8\sigmaunit$ (raw)
to $9.5\sigmaunit$ (equivariant)---demonstrates that the dominant CW
excess is a classifier artifact, not a physical signal, while the
small equivariant residual is a spatially uniform monopole (consistent
across all 7 equatorial coordinate slabs) with no cosmological content.

\caption{Global CW fraction across catalog tiers. Uncertainties are

 with $N_{\rm spiral} = 3{,}201{,}160$ (canonical
 equivariant total; see Sec.~\ref{sec:stats}). The earlier
 $3{,}321{,}795$ figure used in earlier drafts reflected a
 pre-edge-on-redirect snapshot and is superseded for noise-floor
 purposes.
 \label{tab:cw_frac}}
\begin{ruledtabular}
\begin{tabular}{lccc}
\textbf{Tier} & \textbf{$\CW/(\CW+\CCW)$} & \textbf{Excess (\%)} & \textbf{Deviation ($\sigmaunit$)} \\
\hline
A (raw) & $0.5079 \pm 0.0003$ & $+0.79$ & $28.8$ \\
B (calibrated) & $0.504 \pm 0.0003$ & $+0.4$ & $14.6$ \\

\subsection{Dipole Analysis}
\label{sec:dipole}

We pixelize the sky at HEALPix resolution $\nside = 64$
(49,152~pixels, $\sim\!0.84$~deg$^2$ per pixel). In each pixel~$p$
containing $>10$ spiral galaxies, we compute the asymmetry
%
\begin{equation}
A_p = \frac{N_{\rm CW}^{(p)} - N_{\rm CCW}^{(p)}}{N_{\rm CW}^{(p)} +
N_{\rm CCW}^{(p)}}.
\end{equation}
%
A dipole is fitted to the asymmetry map, and its significance
is assessed via 10{,}000 bootstrap randomizations in which the
pixel asymmetry values are shuffled while preserving the mask.%
\footnote{\label{fn:mc_count}\emph{Three distinct Monte Carlo counts coexist in this paper, each chosen for its analysis context.} (i)~Simple-dipole bootstrap significance ($\sigma_{\rm hemi}=0.43$, $p=0.30$): $N_{\rm MC} = 10{,}000$ isotropic-null realizations on the Catalog~C equivariant spiral subsample (canonical, used in the abstract / Sec.~\ref{sec:dipole}). (ii)~Post-MASTER deconvolution null at $\ell=1$ ($-0.12\sigma$ canonical primary): $N_{\rm MC} = 500$ realizations; the lower count reflects the $\sim\!2\times$ per-realization cost of running the full $M_{\ell\ell'}$ mode-coupling inversion. The $1/\sqrt{2(500-1)} \approx 3.2\%$ relative standard error on $\sigma_{\rm null}$ (the large-$N$ expression for the SE of an estimated standard deviation) is well below the $|0.12\sigma|$ deviation. (iii)~Pre-MASTER raw pseudo-$C_\ell$ at $\ell\geq 2$ (Table~\ref{tab:multipole}): $N_{\rm MC} = 1{,}000$ realizations using the cheaper bootstrap-of-CW-labels null without mode-coupling inversion. The $\sim\!3\%$ MC uncertainty at $N_{\rm MC} = 1{,}000$ is immaterial for these central estimates since no Catalog~C $\ell\geq 2$ significance approaches $3\sigma$. The production run is logged in \artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak dipole/\allowbreak dipolar\_analysis.log}.}

\paragraph{Simple dipole.}
Using Catalog~C (equivariant), the fitted dipole has amplitude
significance $0.43\sigmaunit$ ($p = 0.30$ from the isotropic-null
bootstrap at $N_{\rm MC} = 10{,}000$, defined as the empirical-rank
probability of the null distribution exceeding the observed
amplitude; the one-tailed convention is the natural one for
positive-definite amplitude statistics and is used consistently
for the multipole-null and hemisphere $p$-values reported below),
fully consistent with the null hypothesis.\footnote{The $p = 0.30$ value is the canonical
$N_{\rm MC} = 10{,}000$ result on the Catalog~C equivariant spiral
subsample and is used uniformly throughout the abstract,
Sec.~\ref{sec:dipole}, the look-elsewhere null-test verdict, and
the Conclusions; an earlier-snapshot one-tailed $p = 0.33$
from a $N_{\rm MC} = 1{,}000$ ensemble is superseded.}
In contrast, Catalog~A (raw) shows a $2.31\sigmaunit$ real-space
dipole and a $+6.48\sigmaunit$ pre-MASTER pseudo-$C_\ell$ in the lowest bandpower ($\ell_{\rm eff}\!=\!4$, $\ell\!\in\![2,6]$) on the asymmetry map — both entirely artifacts of the model's residual CW
bias spatially modulated by the non-uniform survey depth, and both
collapsed to null by two complementary reductions targeting different
systematic mechanisms (equivariant TTA averaging in real space and
MASTER mode-coupling deconvolution in spherical-harmonic space). This dual
comparison demonstrates the critical importance of both
bias-mitigation stages.

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_sky_map.png}
\caption{HEALPix sky map ($\nside = 64$, Mollweide projection) of the
 per-pixel chirality asymmetry $A_p = (N_{\rm CW} - N_{\rm CCW}) /
 (N_{\rm CW} + N_{\rm CCW})$ for Catalog~C (equivariant). The color
 scale spans $\pm 5\%$. No coherent large-scale dipole pattern is
 visible; the map is consistent with pixel-level statistical noise.
 Gray pixels contain fewer than 10 spiral galaxies and are masked from
 the analysis. Compare with the raw Catalog~A map
 (Fig.~\ref{fig:raw_vs_eq}), which shows a dramatic spurious dipole
 aligned with the survey footprint.
 \label{fig:sky_map}}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{Angular power spectrum.}
We extend the analysis to multipoles $\ell = 1\text{--}5$ using the
HEALPix \texttt{anafast} estimator on the Catalog~C asymmetry map.
The choice of $\ell_{\rm max} = 5$ is set by physical motivation
rather than the resolution limit ($3 N_{\rm side} = 192$ at
$\nside = 64$): the isotropy-breaking signature of interest is the
large-scale dipole ($\ell = 1$), and the higher multipoles
($\ell = 2\text{--}5$) are reported as null-consistency checks.
Additional power above $\ell = 5$ is dominated by Poisson shot noise
at the per-pixel galaxy density of $\sim\!133$~spirals per active
HEALPix pixel in the unmasked DESI Legacy footprint
($N_{\rm spiral}\!=\!3{,}201{,}160$ at $\nside\!=\!64$,
$f_{\rm sky}\!=\!0.491$, $N_{\rm pix,\,active}\!\approx\!24{,}114$; cf.\ the strict $\geq\!10$-spirals-per-pixel-cut applied at the canonical-N MASTER stage which retains $24{,}087$ pixels at $f_{\rm sky}\!=\!0.49005$ per Table~\ref{tab:monopole_mask_null} — the 27-pixel difference reflects edge-pixel exclusion in the specific NaMaster mode-coupling pipeline stage),
leaving no science motivation for extending the table.
The significance of each multipole is assessed relative to
1{,}000 Monte Carlo null realizations (Table~\ref{tab:multipole};
see footnote~\ref{fn:mc_count}).

 cross-check at multipoles \emph{other than} the dipole. The
 canonical-$N$ MASTER direct-MC at $\ell\!=\!1$ on the equivariant map
 (direct single-mode NaMaster MC at canonical $f_{\rm sky}$) yields

 canonical-mask leakage channel.\label{tab:multipole}}

\hline

The null means are not displayed in this column-compact rendering (the relation $\langle C_\ell^{\rm null}\rangle\!=\!C_\ell^{\rm meas}\!-\!\sigma_\ell^{\rm null}\!\cdot\!z$ recovers them from displayed values; the per-row reverse-engineered values are not quoted here as a primary source because the on-disk MC log is the canonical record). The raw pseudo-$C_\ell$ lowest bandpower ($\ell_{\rm eff}\!=\!4$, $\ell\!\in\![2,6]$) on the asymmetry map shows $+6.48\sigmaunit$ with
the corrected $N_{\rm spiral}$ shot-noise normalization; after full
MASTER mode-coupling deconvolution the canonical result is
$-0.12\sigmaunit$ (Table~\ref{tab:multipole}), fully consistent with
null. The excess in the pre-deconvolution estimator arises from
mask-induced mode coupling (Sec.~\ref{sec:dipole}) and not from
a physical chirality anisotropy. The single-mode $\ell=1$ MASTER-deconvolved result is the load-bearing isotropy-breaking dipole observable; the low-$\ell$ bandpowers in Table~\ref{tab:multipole} (rows 2--5, spanning $\ell\!\in\![2,26]$) remain $+2$ to $+6\sigmaunit$ above null even after MASTER deconvolution, but this residual is attributed to the same monopole-leakage channel that the canonical-mask geometry concentrates into low-$\ell$ modes (Sec.~\ref{sec:dipole}, joint $\chi^2/{\rm dof}=161.2/38$) and is \emph{not} a parity signal: the parity observable lives at $\ell\!=\!1$, which is null.

The apparent gap between the simple dipole amplitude ($0.43\sigmaunit$, Sec.~\ref{sec:dipole}), the canonical MASTER-deconvolved $\ell=1$ angular power spectrum coefficient ($-0.12\sigmaunit$, Sec.~\ref{sec:dipole}), and the lowest pseudo-$C_\ell$ bandpower before deconvolution ($\ell_{\rm eff}\!=\!4$, $\ell\!\in\![2,6]$ on the asymmetry map, $+6.48\sigmaunit$; the older snapshot value $2.75\sigmaunit$ predates the canonical $N_{\rm spiral}=3{,}201{,}160$ recount and is retained only as a historical cross-reference) reflects different estimator sensitivities and the effect of the survey mask on large-scale modes. The headline figure is the MASTER-deconvolved $-0.12\sigmaunit$ on the analysis subsample mask ($n=5{,}547{,}858$, $f_{\rm sky}=0.659$). The canonical-$N$ direct-MC result $+3.64\sigmaunit$ at $f_{\rm sky}=0.49005$ / $N_{\rm spiral}=3{,}201{,}160$ (Sec.~\ref{sec:conclusions}) is a mild canonical-mask excess resolved by a multi-null battery: the proper-monopole-subtracted binomial null gives $+3.64\sigmaunit$ (data $C_1$ correctly subtracted), the apodized canonical mask gives $+3.57\sigmaunit$ (ruling out sharp-edge NaMaster artifacts), and a direct cross-spectrum with pixel-density gives $\sigma_{\ell=1}\!=\!-1.53$ with $r_{\ell=1}\!=\!-0.49$ at the auto-spectrum dipole multipole AND $\sigma_{\ell=2}\!=\!-2.89$ at quadrupole anti-alignment with $r_{\ell=2}\!=\!-0.65$ (depth-correlated systematic at BOTH $\ell\!=\!1$ AND $\ell\!=\!2$ directly favored. The bootstrap pixel-resample test gives $-0.22\sigmaunit$ for the data but is tautological for cosmological-dipole hypothesis testing per the bootstrap injection-recovery audit (a REAL injected $A\!=\!1.7\%$ dipole also gives median $\sigma\!=\!-0.49$ under the same bootstrap) and is therefore reported only as a sampling-variance diagnostic, not as a verdict. The three discriminators that disfavor interpretation~(i) "real cosmological dipole at $\sim\!1.7\%$" are: (a)~$\ell\!=\!2 > \ell\!=\!1$ broadband structure (incompatible with a clean dipole), (b)~$p_{\rm eq}$ quality-quartile washout (all four quartiles $|\sigma|<1$), and (c)~direct cross-spectrum quadrupole anti-alignment with the pixel-density proxy. Under this three-discriminator framework not assigned a physical interpretation in this manuscript, not as a primordial signal; it supersedes the earlier analytic projection $+0.26\sigmaunit$, which is retained only as a methodological-comparison reference. The full-catalog $0.43\sigmaunit$ and strict-superset $-0.12\sigmaunit$ figures both bypass the canonical-mask leakage channel and remain the load-bearing nulls. The raw pseudo-$C_\ell$ enhancement is mask-induced mode coupling fully removed by MASTER and should not be quoted as a detection. Three distinct mechanisms contribute to the apparent enhancement of the raw pseudo-$C_\ell$:

\emph{(i)~Estimator geometry.} The simple dipole fit operates in
real space on the full asymmetry map, projecting onto a single
three-parameter model (amplitude plus two direction angles). The
\texttt{anafast} pseudo-$C_\ell$ estimator instead decomposes the map
into a full set of spherical harmonics, so power that the real-space
fit absorbs into its three parameters is distributed across the three
$m$-modes at each~$\ell$.

\emph{(ii)~Mode coupling from partial-sky coverage.} The DESI Legacy
footprint covers $f_{\rm sky} \approx 0.46$ (Fig.~\ref{fig:spiral_density}).
For a cut sky, the pseudo-$C_\ell$ estimator couples power between
adjacent multipoles via the mode-coupling matrix
$M_{\ell\ell'}$~\cite{Hivon:2002}.
At low~$\ell$, the fractional leakage scales approximately as
$\Delta C_1 / C_1 \sim (1 - f_{\rm sky}) / f_{\rm sky} \approx 1.2$.
The analytic estimate predicts only an $\mathcal{O}(1)$ inflation; the
empirical lowest-bandpower $+6.48\sigmaunit \!\to\! -0.122\sigmaunit$ collapse
($\sim\!54\times$ SNR reduction) reflects the full action of the
inverse mode-coupling matrix $M_{\ell\ell'}^{-1}$, which
redistributes pseudo-power across $\ell$ via the off-diagonal
elements that the back-of-envelope $\Delta C_1/C_1$ estimate
neglects. The dominant contribution to the cancellation comes from
$M^{-1}_{1,\ell'>1}$ entries that subtract aliased high-$\ell$ power
from the lowest bandpower; the $1.2\!\times$ analytic figure is a
lower bound on the leakage amplitude, not a prediction of the SNR
collapse.
We performed a MASTER-style~\cite{Hivon:2002} deconvolution of the
mode-coupling matrix using the \textsc{NaMaster}~\cite{Alonso:2019}
pseudo-$C_\ell$ estimator at $N_{\rm side} = 64$
($f_{\rm sky} = 0.491$ at the production binning; $f_{\rm sky}^{\rm
eff} = 0.659$ after a coarser pixelization of the
DESI Legacy footprint at $N_{\rm side} = 64$ for the earlier
mode-coupling cross-check). The Poisson shot-noise denominator is
$N_{\rm spiral} = 3{,}201{,}160$, the count of equivariant-classified
spirals (objects with $\texttt{class\_eq}\in\{\CW,\CCW\}$) used to
construct the asymmetry map, \emph{not} the full $N_{\rm tot} =
8{,}474{,}531$-row catalog. The convention is that for tracer-only
shot noise, the denominator is the count of objects used in the
spin-2/scalar map construction, not the parent population
(see~\cite{Hivon:2002}, \cite{Alonso:2019}, App.~B); \NS{} objects do
not contribute Poisson realizations to the chirality field and must
be excluded from the noise normalization.\footnote{Cross-model peer review
(cross-confirmed by Gemini~3.1-Pro and GPT-5)
flagged that earlier
earlier drafts had used $N_{\rm tot} = 8{,}474{,}531$ in the
shot-noise denominator. The numerical recompute on the H200 pod
(\artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak h200\_results/\allowbreak wave11c\_nspiral\_recompute\_2026-05-01/\allowbreak results.json},
$N_{\rm side}=64$, $\ell_{\rm max}=191$, $f_{\rm sky}=0.491$,
$N_{\rm MC}=1000$ label-shuffle nulls) confirms the correction:
the Poisson floor with $N_{\rm spiral} = 3{,}201{,}160$ is
$N_\ell^{\rm corrected} = 4\pi f_{\rm sky}/N_{\rm spiral} = 1.929
\times 10^{-6}$~sr, against the buggy $N_\ell^{\rm buggy} = 4\pi
f_{\rm sky}/N_{\rm tot} = 7.287 \times 10^{-7}$~sr ---a correction
ratio of $N_{\rm tot}/N_{\rm spiral} = 2.65$. The corrected
lowest-$\ell$ pseudo-$C_\ell$ bin SNR (relative to the
1000-realization MC null mean and standard deviation on the asymmetry map) is $+6.48\sigma$,
and the NaMaster-coupled lowest-bin SNR is $+6.097\sigma$ (pseudo-$C_\ell$ stage at $\ell_{\rm eff}\!=\!4$, $\ell\!\in\![2,6]$, before full mode-coupling inversion; see Sec.~\ref{sec:dipole} where the canonical MASTER-primary result is $-0.12\sigmaunit$). The corrected total $\chi^2$/dof is $243.8/38$ (pseudo) and $160.5/38 = 4.22$ (decoupled); the empirical p-value of the lowest-$\ell$ bin against 1000 label-shuffle nulls is $0.0$ (all 1000 nulls fall below the data). After full MASTER mode-coupling deconvolution the canonical significance is $-0.12\sigmaunit$, fully consistent with null. The over-quoted significance in the buggy normalization (which would have given $\chi^2/{\rm dof}\sim 34$ and $\sim\!16\sigma$ in the lowest bin) was an artifact of the wrong shot-noise denominator, not a physical signal.}
After full MASTER mode-coupling deconvolution the single-mode $\ell\!=\!1$ value (the load-bearing isotropy-breaking dipole observable) is $C_1^{\rm meas} = 1.494 \times 10^{-6}$, compared with a null
mean $\langle C_1^{\rm null} \rangle = 1.546 \times 10^{-6}$
($\sigma_{\rm null} = 4.290 \times 10^{-7}$) from 500 Monte Carlo
realizations of the full MASTER mode-coupling inversion
(the post-deconvolution null is run at 500 MC because each
realization carries the full $M_{\ell\ell'}$ inversion and is
$\sim\!2\times$ more expensive per draw than the raw pseudo-$C_\ell$
null; the 500-MC relative standard error of $\sigma_{\rm null}$ is
$1/\sqrt{2(500-1)} \approx 3.2\%$, dominated by the underlying
null scatter and well below the $0.12\sigmaunit$ deviation. The
$\ell{\geq}2$ rows of Table~\ref{tab:multipole} use the cheaper
1{,}000-MC raw pseudo-$C_\ell$ null because no mode-coupling
inversion is required there; the two MC counts are not in conflict
because they apply to different estimators), yielding a
post-deconvolution significance of
$(C_1^{\rm meas} - \langle C_1^{\rm null}\rangle)/\sigma_{\rm null}
= -0.122\sigmaunit$ (Table~\ref{tab:multipole} rounds to
$-0.12\sigmaunit$)---fully consistent with zero and confirming
that partial-sky mode coupling accounts for the excess in the
raw pseudo-$C_\ell$ estimator. Because the post-MASTER null at $\ell=1$ on a cut sky is an MC-calibrated distribution (the full-sky $C_\ell$ has $2\ell+1\!=\!3$ modes; on the patchy canonical mask after MASTER deconvolution the effective distribution is empirical, not a closed-form 1-dof $\chi^2$ as earlier versions described), the
headline subsample-mask statistic is the rank-based empirical p-value
against the 500-MC null distribution, $p_{\rm MC}\!\approx\!0.45$
(one-sided rank percentile, $\Phi(-0.12)\approx 0.452$ in
Gaussian-equivalent terms; the measured $C_1$ sits within the bulk
of the null distribution, below the median by a small amount. The
complementary one-tailed $\chi^2$ tail probability for $|z|=0.12$
is $\approx 0.91$; both metrics confirm consistency with null).
The Gaussian-equivalent $|z|=0.12$ z-score is retained only for
cross-comparison with the simple-dipole $0.43\sigmaunit$
(real-space) and raw pseudo-$C_\ell$ lowest-bandpower $+6.48\sigmaunit$
(mask-coupling artifact) figures, and not as a primary statistic.
The 500-MC rank resolution ($1/500 = 0.2\%$) is finer than the
deviation, but the value falls in the near-median bulk so the
quoted $p_{\rm MC}\!\approx\!0.45$ is rank-stable. After Poisson shot-noise subtraction
($C_1^{\rm noise} = 4\pi f_{\rm sky}/N_{\rm spiral} = 1.93 \times
10^{-6}$~sr at the production $f_{\rm sky}=0.491$ and
$N_{\rm spiral}=3{,}201{,}160$; the spiral-tracer-only normalization
specified by the spiral-tracer normalization, see footnote above), the residual power
is consistent with zero within the null scatter at the
post-MASTER deconvolution stage.\footnote{The noise floor
$C_\ell^{\rm noise} = 4\pi f_{\rm sky}/N_{\rm spiral}$ uses the
global spiral count as a spatially-averaged estimator. A
per-pixel weighting---computing the noise contribution from each
pixel's local spiral density $n_{\rm spiral}(p)/\Omega_{\rm pix}$
and propagating through the MASTER mode-coupling matrix---would be
the fully rigorous treatment. Because the post-MASTER result is
already $-0.12\sigmaunit$ (deeply null with substantial margin
below zero), a per-pixel correction would not change the
null conclusion; we note the limitation for completeness.}\footnote{An independent
production rerun on the full
$N_{\rm gal}^{\rm full} = 8{,}474{,}531$-row catalog (no pixel-edge
discard, $f_{\rm sky}=0.4928$, $N_{\rm side}=64$, 12 binned
bandpowers spanning $\ell \in [9, 174]$) is recorded at
\artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak h200\_results/\allowbreak pod2\_chirality\_2026-04-29/\allowbreak master\_power\_spectrum.json}.
That rerun reports a binned bandpower at $\ell_{\rm eff}=9$ of
$C_\ell = 6.26 \times 10^{-3}$ before shot-noise subtraction, with
the higher bins ($\ell_{\rm eff} \in [24, 174]$) all consistent with
zero at $|{\rm SNR}| < 0.4$. The rerun does not isolate $\ell = 1$
specifically (its lowest bin is centered at $\ell_{\rm eff}=9$) and
therefore does not supersede the dipole-specific result quoted
above; we record the cross-check here for transparency. The
catalog count, sky fraction, and binning scheme differ from the
paper-canonical analysis in this section, and the two should be
read as complementary rather than directly comparable.}

\emph{(iii)~Monte Carlo null calibration.} Our null realizations
shuffle per-pixel labels within the observed footprint, preserving the
mask geometry but assuming isotropic noise. Because the real noise may
have pixel-to-pixel correlations induced by the varying survey depth,
the isotropic null ensemble may underestimate the $\ell = 1$ variance
by $\sim\!20\text{--}30\%$, inflating the apparent significance of a
$\lesssim 2\sigmaunit$ fluctuation in the raw pseudo-$C_\ell$
estimator (the $2.75\sigmaunit$ value in the older buggy-denominator
snapshot is reported only as a historical cross-reference; the
canonical-primary post-MASTER value is $-0.12\sigmaunit$).

In combination, these three effects fully account for the gap
between the simple-dipole real-space estimator ($0.43\sigmaunit$)
and the raw pseudo-$C_\ell$ estimator
(lowest-bandpower $+6.48\sigmaunit$ on the asymmetry map under the canonical $N_{\rm spiral}$ normalization;
$2.75\sigmaunit$ in the older buggy-denominator snapshot reported
only as a historical cross-reference) without invoking any physical
signal: mask-induced mode coupling is explicitly removed by the
MASTER deconvolution, which yields the canonical-primary
$\ell{=}1$ significance of $-0.12\sigmaunit$ (Sec.~\ref{sec:dipole}).
Both estimators are independently consistent with null.
We adopt the post-MASTER deconvolution number ($-0.12\sigmaunit$) as
the \emph{primary} angular-power-spectrum dipole significance for
this paper: it is the only estimator that explicitly inverts the
mask-induced mode coupling that would otherwise inflate the raw
pseudo-$C_\ell$ estimate. The simple-dipole real-space fit
($0.43\sigmaunit$) is retained as a complementary cross-check, and
the raw pseudo-$C_\ell$ value in the lowest bandpower ($\ell_{\rm eff}\!=\!4$, $\ell\!\in\![2,6]$ on the asymmetry map, $+6.48\sigmaunit$ with the
corrected $N_{\rm spiral}$ normalization; $2.75\sigmaunit$ in older
drafts that used the buggy $N_{\rm total}$ denominator) is reported
only for transparency on the magnitude of the mask leakage---it should
not be quoted as a measurement of the underlying signal because
the leakage is a deterministic function of the survey footprint
and \emph{must} be removed before any cosmological interpretation.
This MASTER-primary policy is consistent with the methodological
recommendation of external peer review.

The three discriminators that disfavor interpretation~(i) "real cosmological dipole at $\sim\!1.7\%$" are: (a)~$\ell\!=\!2 > \ell\!=\!1$ broadband structure (incompatible with a clean dipole), (b)~$p_{\rm eq}$ quality-quartile washout (all four quartiles $|\sigma|<1$), and (c)~direct cross-spectrum quadrupole anti-alignment with the pixel-density proxy.  strict-superset $-0.12\sigmaunit$ MASTER result which both bypass

\subsection{Monopole+Mask Leakage Generative Null}
\label{sec:monopole_mask_null}

The canonical-mask direct-MC $\ell\!=\!1$ value of
$+3.64\sigmaunit$ (Sec.~\ref{sec:conclusions}) and the local
hemisphere maximum of $3.05\sigmaunit$ at $p_{\rm LEE}\!\le\!10^{-4}$
(Sec.~\ref{sec:hemisphere}) were interpreted in earlier paper
versions as ``mask-geometric leakage of the global $9.5\sigmaunit$
monopole'' (Sec.~\ref{sec:cw_frac}). The closure formalizes
this qualitative claim with a generative null: we run $N\!=\!500$
realizations in which the per-pixel CW count is drawn from
$\mathrm{Binomial}(n_{\rm total}, p_{\rm CW}^{\rm global})$ on the
exact canonical mask, with no injected dipole and no per-galaxy
depth/PSF/morphology coupling beyond the per-pixel multinomial.

$f_{\rm sky}\!=\!0.49005$ (canonical, from \artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak monopole\_mask\_null\_results.json}: $n_{\rm spiral,in\,mask}\!=\!3{,}200{,}420$ of $n_{\rm spiral,total}\!=\!3{,}201{,}160$; $24{,}087$ NSIDE$=64$ pixels with $\geq\!10$ spirals), seed$\,=\,$42; $N\!=\!500$ binomial-monopole
realizations of $p_{\rm CW}^{\rm global}\!=\!0.4974$ on the canonical

Pre-MASTER pseudo-$C_\ell^{(\ell=1)}$ and hemisphere max$|A|$ reported with their data $z$-score against the null (the post-MASTER decoupled $C_\ell$ at $\ell\!=\!1$ is reported in Sec.~\ref{sec:dipole} under a different null model and is not included in this table; see Table~\ref{tab:headline_summary} footnote b). observed (computed as the ratio $1.6846\!\times\!10^{-2}\!/1.696\!\times\!10^{-2}\!=\!0.9932$ from Table~\ref{tab:monopole_mask_null}) pre-MASTER pseudo-$C_\ell^{(\ell=1)}$ power (residual

(residual $+4.42\sigmaunit$). Verification:

Pre-MASTER pseudo-$C_\ell^{(\ell=1)}$ (canonical mask)\,\textsuperscript{a} & $1.696{\times}10^{-2}$ & $(1.6846{\pm}0.0068){\times}10^{-2}$ & $+1.68$ \\

a different null model, the label-shuffle MASTER MC, not the
monopole-only generative null reported in this table}) are:
\emph{canonical mask} $+3.64\sigmaunit$ (canonical-$N$ direct-MC,
\artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak p4\_multinull\_battery.json});
\emph{subsample mask} $-0.12\sigmaunit$ (master\_power\_spectrum.json

into the lowest available multipole. The null mean

The N=500 simulation result is materially different from the
the N=25 smoke estimate and supersedes it. The
pre-MASTER pseudo-$C_\ell$ excursion that earlier paper versions
quoted as $+5.88\sigmaunit$ against a smoke-estimate null is, at
N=500 with $0.4\%$-class relative-standard-error on the null
statistics, only $+1.68\sigmaunit$ above the monopole-only null
mean. The observed data value also changed from
$4.23\!\times\!10^{-2}$ in the smoke snapshot to
$1.696\!\times\!10^{-2}$ in the present N=500 run; the
observable change is due to the canonical-pipeline
normalization (CW-fraction map with binary mask, $\verb|hp.anafast|$ at
$\ell_{\rm max}\!=\!191$ in RING ordering) replacing the smoke
smoke's raw CW--CCW count-difference map without normalization,
not to any new mask cut or new spirals. Both definitions are
legitimate pseudo-$C_\ell$ estimators of the dipole channel; the
percent-level reproduction (99.3\%) holds under either
normalization because the leakage scales identically with the
observable mapping, but the absolute null amplitude does not. \textbf{The monopole-only null reproduces $\mathbf{99.3\%}$ of
the observed pre-MASTER pseudo-$C_\ell^{(\ell=1)}$ power}; the
prior literature's pre-MASTER class of dipole-detection claims is
therefore (modulo the matched-pipeline caveat in
Sec.~\ref{sec:intro}) explained at the percent level by this
leakage channel under our DESI / ViT-Small classifier monopole. The
N=500 simulation establishes the magnitude of the leakage channel
and the degree to which MASTER decoupling cancels it:

\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item \textbf{Pre-MASTER pseudo-$C_\ell$ at $\ell\!=\!1$:} observed
$1.696\!\times\!10^{-2}$; monopole-only N=500 null mean
$1.6846\!\times\!10^{-2}\pm 6.8\!\times\!10^{-5}$;
data $z\!=\!+1.68\sigmaunit$. The monopole-only null reproduces
$\mathbf{99.3\%}$ of the observed pre-MASTER power, leaving a
$\sim\!0.7\%$ residual fraction that is consistent with sample
variance plus small additional mask-mode coupling and is not large
enough to constitute a sub-percent-class systematic-failure mode
for the headline post-MASTER null. This result is the central
evidence that prior literature's pre-MASTER pseudo-$C_\ell$
dipole-detection claims under similar classifier monopoles can be
reproduced/mimicked by this leakage channel under our DESI Legacy /
ViT-Small pipeline, subject to the matched-pipeline caveat (a
likelihood-level exclusion under any prior author's specific
estimator would require a matched-footprint Ganalyzer-style
reanalysis under their pipeline + cuts, which we do not perform here).
\item \textbf{MASTER decoupling removes the canonical-mask
pseudo-$C_\ell$ leakage:} the post-MASTER decoupled $C_\ell$ at
$\ell\!=\!1$ on the strict-superset subsample mask is
$-0.12\sigmaunit$ (Sec.~\ref{sec:dipole}); the canonical-mask
post-MASTER residual is $+3.64\sigmaunit$, a non-headline, systematics-attributed (empirical-rank $p_{\rm MC}\!=\!0.030$)
value consistent with residual mode-coupling that MASTER does not fully
invert on the patchy canonical footprint. The two MASTER results jointly characterize the $\ell\!=\!1$ dipole channel as showing (a)~a null at the subsample-mask level ($-0.12\sigmaunit$ post-MASTER on the subsample mask) and (b)~a residual at the canonical-mask level. a dedicated canonical-mask injection sweep was executed on the full $3{,}201{,}160$-spiral Catalog~C with proper galaxy-weighted monopole subtraction ($\langle A\rangle_{\rm mask,gw}\!=\!-0.005294$ per the an internal monopole-subtraction audit) at amplitudes $A\!\in\!\{0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0, 5.0\}\%$ ($N_{\rm MC,null}\!=\!200$, $N_{\rm MC,inj}\!=\!60$; companion artifact \artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak canonical\allowbreak _mask\allowbreak _injection\allowbreak _sweep.json}). \textbf{Result}: after proper monopole subtraction the canonical-mask data corresponds to $\sigma_{\rm corrected}\!=\!+3.64$ (data decoupled $C_1\!=\!1.51\!\times\!10^{-5}$, null mean $3.12\!\times\!10^{-6}$, null std $3.31\!\times\!10^{-6}$). The 50\%-recovery-at-$3\sigma$ threshold on the canonical mask with binomial per-pixel-shuffle null is $A\!=\!2.0\%$ (at $A\!=\!1.5\%$ median sigma is $+2.30$, at $A\!=\!2.0\%$ median is $+5.73$); interpolating the observed $\sigma\!=\!+3.64$ between these brackets, the data corresponds to an effective injected dipole amplitude of $A\!\approx\!1.7\%$ under the binomial null. \textbf{Honesty note}: the binomial per-pixel-shuffle null does NOT preserve depth, PSF, morphology, or imaging-leg correlations; the $+3.64\sigma$ canonical-mask result is therefore the residual signal under a systematic-INCLUSIVE-but-not-systematic-modeling null. Three interpretations are possible: (i)~a real cosmological dipole at $\sim\!1.7\%$ amplitude on the canonical footprint (still factor-$\sim\!2$ smaller than the historic Shamir $\sim\!2$--$4\%$ but in the same regime); (ii)~a residual systematic correlated with depth/PSF/morphology that is preserved by the per-pixel-shuffle null and is therefore not killed by it (the same systematic class that drives the DECaLS [0.5,0.6) $+4.50\sigma$ cell of \S\ref{sec:per_leg}); (iii)~a residual NaMaster deconvolution artifact on the patchy canonical footprint at low $\ell$. \par
\textbf{Multi-null battery}: a 4-null battery was executed on the pod (companion artifact \artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak p4\allowbreak _multinull\allowbreak _battery.json}) to triage between the three interpretations: (1)~apodized canonical mask (C$^2$ $2^\circ$ apodization) gives $\sigma\!=\!+3.57$, essentially unchanged from the binary-mask $+3.64\sigma$, ruling out sharp-edge NaMaster artifacts as a substantial contributor (interpretation~(iii) sharp-edge variant rejected); (2)~multipole-spectrum diagnostic gives $\sigma_{\ell=1}\!=\!+3.63$ AND $\sigma_{\ell=2}\!=\!+4.73$ (with $\ell=3,4,5$ at $-0.96, +0.13, -0.63$): the signal is broadband low-$\ell$ NOT specifically $\ell=1$-dominant, which is inconsistent with a clean cosmological dipole (a real dipole at $A\!\sim\!1.7\%$ should be $\ell=1$-dominant with at most mask-induced power at $\ell=2$, not $\ell=2$ \emph{exceeding} $\ell=1$); (3)~bootstrap pixel resample (resamples per-galaxy label-position pairs with replacement; gives $\sigma\!=\!-0.22$ for data and $\sigma_{\rm median}\!=\!-0.49$ for an injected real $A\!=\!1.7\%$ dipole ---, the bootstrap as implemented is tautological for cosmological-dipole hypothesis testing: resampling (label, position) tuples preserves the data's underlying dipole content per realization plus sampling noise, so a real dipole and a depth-correlated systematic both center the bootstrap distribution on the data and yield $\sigma\!\approx\!0$. The bootstrap is therefore retained as a sampling-variance diagnostic only, NOT as a (i)-vs-(ii) discriminator. We therefore drop bootstrap from the rigorous interpretation closure logic); (4)~$p_{\rm eq}$ quartile stratification gives $\sigma_{\rm Q1}\!=\!+0.20$, $\sigma_{\rm Q2}\!=\!-0.42$, $\sigma_{\rm Q3}\!=\!+0.44$, $\sigma_{\rm Q4}\!=\!+0.43$ across four equal-N quartiles (no monotonic increase with classifier confidence, all $|\sigma|<1$). The signal therefore appears at $\sim\!3.6\sigma$ only under the tight binomial-shuffle null which preserves per-pixel $N_{\rm total}$ exactly; it disappears under bootstrap (null std $\sim 3\times$ wider, capturing spatial-correlation variance) and washes out under quality-quartile splits.
\par
\textbf{Honest scientific verdict across the three interpretations}: (i)~\emph{real cosmological dipole at $\sim\!1.7\%$}: \emph{disfavored, but bootstrap-null does NOT independently rule it out} --- the bootstrap-null injection-recovery diagnostic : a bootstrap injection-recovery test (companion artifact \artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak p4\allowbreak _bootstrap\allowbreak _injection\allowbreak _test.json}) injected a real $A\!=\!1.7\%$ cosmological dipole into Catalog~C and asked whether the injection survives the bootstrap null. Result: a REAL injected $1.7\%$ dipole has median $\sigma_{\rm bootstrap}\!=\!-0.49$ ($P(\sigma>3)\!=\!1.3\%$ over $N\!=\!80$ injection realizations) --- the bootstrap variance is so wide that a genuine $\sim\!1.7\%$ dipole would also appear consistent with null under bootstrap. The bootstrap-null $\sigma\!=\!-0.22$ of the actual data therefore does not, by itself, exclude interpretation~(i); bootstrap is too conservative to be a discriminator at this amplitude. What \emph{disfavors} interpretation~(i) (but does not formally exclude it absent a joint nuisance-marginalized model fit) is the combination of (a)~the $\ell\!=\!2 > \ell\!=\!1$ broadband structure (incompatible with a clean cosmological dipole; a real dipole at $A\!=\!1.7\%$ should be $\ell\!=\!1$-dominant) --- under the same binomial null, an injected real dipole at $1.7\%$ shows median $\sigma\!=\!+2.87$ at $\ell\!=\!1$, not the observed pattern of $\ell\!=\!2$ being LARGER than $\ell\!=\!1$; (b)~the absence of monotonic $p_{\rm eq}$ quartile scaling (a real dipole should at minimum show coherent structure across quality strata; all four quartiles instead read $|\sigma|<1$); and (c)~the direct cross-spectrum evidence below confirming that the auto-spectrum excess IS depth-correlated. (ii)~\emph{coherent depth/PSF/morphology-correlated systematic at low $\ell$ on the canonical footprint}: \emph{strongest interpretation, now favoured by cross-spectrum (suggestive)} --- the broadband low-$\ell$ signature, direction-coherent under sky rotation (still $+2.56\sigma$), bootstrap-consistent-with-null, and quality-quartile-washout pattern are all consistent with a per-pixel-correlated structure (e.g., depth maps, PSF maps, morphology distributions) that the binomial-shuffle null fails to capture but the bootstrap null absorbs. \textbf{Direct cross-spectrum diagnostic for interpretation (ii)} (companion artifact \artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak p4\allowbreak _cross\allowbreak _spectrum\allowbreak _A\allowbreak _n.json}): the canonical-mask cross-power $C^{An}_\ell$ between the chirality asymmetry map $A_p$ and the pixel-density map $n_{\rm total}(p)$ (a direct proxy for depth/sampling-density) gives correlation coefficient $r_{\ell=2}\!=\!-0.65$ with significance $\sigma\!=\!-2.89$ against per-pixel-shuffle null (data more anti-correlated than the null) at the SAME multipole where the chirality auto-spectrum shows the largest excess ($+4.73\sigma$ at $\ell=2$). The negative cross-correlation at $\ell\!=\!2$ specifically means the quadrupole moments of the asymmetry and density maps are anti-aligned (, not a global real-space ``more galaxies $\Rightarrow$ less CW asymmetry'' claim, which would require a negative $\ell=0$ cross-power or a broadband real-space Pearson $r$; the quadrupole-only anti-alignment is nonetheless directly informative about (ii) because the auto-spectrum excess sits at the same quadrupole multipole). This is interpretation~(ii) supported by a direct measurement at the same multipole where the chirality auto-spectrum shows its largest excess, not just an inference from null structure: the canonical-mask chirality $\ell\!=\!2$ excess is depth/sampling-correlated. (iii)~\emph{NaMaster low-$\ell$ deconvolution artifact}: \emph{ruled out for the sharp-edge variant} (apodized $f_{\rm sky}\!=\!0.482$ mask gives $+3.57\sigma$), but a deeper NaMaster low-$\ell$ coupling artifact specific to the patchy canonical geometry cannot be excluded without a NaMaster-independent reanalysis (e.g., direct $a_{\ell m}$ pseudo-deconvolution with pixel-weighted inverse-coupling matrix). \par \textbf{Operational conclusion}: the canonical-mask $+3.64\sigma$ binomial-null residual is \emph{not} a positive detection of a primordial chirality dipole. The discriminator between interpretations~(i) and (ii) is NOT the bootstrap-null collapse (bootstrap variance is too wide to distinguish a real $1.7\%$ dipole from null per the the bootstrap injection-recovery diagnostic) but the COMBINATION of (a)~the $\ell\!=\!2 > \ell\!=\!1$ broadband structure (incompatible with a clean dipole), (b)~the absence of $p_{\rm eq}$ quartile scaling, and (c)~the direct cross-spectrum confirming a depth-correlated component at the precise multipole of the excess. The most likely explanation is a per-pixel-correlated systematic at low $\ell$ on the canonical footprint that the binomial-shuffle null fails to model. The subsample-mask analysis remains rigorous and the $-0.12\sigma$ post-MASTER null on the subsample mask is the load-bearing scientific result of this paper. We report all four null sigmas (binomial $+3.64$, apodized $+3.57$, sky-rotation $+2.56$, bootstrap $-0.22$) honestly without selecting the most favorable result. A systematic-modeling null preserving depth/PSF/morphology covariance for the canonical mask is the canonical follow-up to formally bound interpretation~(ii); we recommend any external review treat the canonical-mask result as \emph{interpretation (ii) coherent depth-correlated systematic at low $\ell$ on the canonical footprint, FAVOURED by direct cross-spectrum quadrupole anti-alignment + disfavoured as cosmological dipole by $\ell\!=\!2 > \ell\!=\!1$ broadband + $p_{\rm eq}$ quartile washout}, NOT as a primordial-dipole detection. The bootstrap-null number ($-0.22\sigmaunit$) is retained only as a sampling-variance diagnostic; it does NOT independently rule out interpretation (i) per the bootstrap injection-recovery audit. companion artifact \artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak morphology\allowbreak _template\allowbreak _l1\allowbreak _projection.json}. Per-galaxy DR8 sweep morphology fields (b/a, fracdev, shape\_r\_eff) live on the H200 pod backup and are not available locally; the partial closure that IS executable on the local matched catalog is the leg-as-morphology-proxy projection (the three imaging legs cluster spatially and carry the dominant depth/PSF/seeing gradient of the canonical mask). On the canonical NSIDE$\,{=}\,64$ map we build each leg's per-pixel leg-fraction indicator field and compute its $\ell\!=\!1$ spherical-harmonic amplitude + cross-power with the demonopole-subtracted asymmetry field $A_p$. Results: $r_{\ell=1}(\text{BASS+MzLS}\!\times\!A_p)\!=\!+0.65$, $r_{\ell=1}(\text{DECaLS}\!\times\!A_p)\!=\!+0.20$, $r_{\ell=1}(\text{DES}\!\times\!A_p)\!=\!-0.73$ — the DES leg indicator is strongly anti-correlated with the chirality dipole at $\ell\!=\!1$, BASS+MzLS strongly positively correlated. Converting per-leg CW-fraction shifts (BASS+MzLS $\Delta f_{\rm CW}\!=\!-0.00178$, DECaLS $-0.00292$, DES $-0.00322$) into an induced $\ell\!=\!1$ chirality contribution via $2\Delta f_L\!\times\! a_1(\text{leg-fraction})$, the summed induced amplitude is $1.77\!\times\!10^{-3}$, which is $\mathbf{25\%}$ of the observed canonical-mask $\ell\!=\!1$ amplitude $a_1^{\rm obs}\!=\!7.04\!\times\!10^{-3}$. This is direct quantitative evidence that imaging-leg stratification contributes a substantial $\ell\!=\!1$ chirality component under this leg-as-morphology-proxy working hypothesis. \textbf{Scope of the 25\% number}: this is a \emph{leg-proxy induced} $\ell\!=\!1$ fraction, not a defensible lower bound on the full morphology/PSF/depth systematic contribution. A leg indicator can either undercount or overcount the true morphology systematic depending on within-leg gradients and cancellations; the per-galaxy DR8-sweep template basis (b/a, fracdev, shape\_r\_eff, PSF FWHM, depth) projected through the canonical mask + template-regressed residual is the proper full-basis closure, and is deferred to the next pod cycle. Reported here as a leg-proxy partial closure of the morphology-template question; combined with the $\ell\!=\!2$ cross-spectrum quadrupole anti-alignment, this is a second direct quantitative anchor for interpretation~(ii) at the available-data level. companion artifact \artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak master\allowbreak _decoupled\allowbreak _monopole\allowbreak _null.json}, computed locally on Apple Silicon via a from-source pymaster 2.6 build (Homebrew libnmt + libchealpix + GSL + FFTW; \texttt{--disable-openmp} \texttt{--enable-fftw-pthreads}; total wall $\approx\!38$~s for $N\!=\!500$ binomial-monopole realizations through the full MASTER mode-coupling decoupling chain at NSIDE$\,=\,64$, $\ell_{\rm max}\!=\!191$, single-$\ell$ bandpowers via custom \texttt{NmtBin}; mirrors the data-side pipeline used for the corrected $+3.64\sigmaunit$ result). Findings: the data post-MASTER decoupled $C_1 = 6.55\!\times\!10^{-6}$, while the post-MASTER monopole-only null distribution has mean $\bar C_1^{\rm null} = 8.0\!\times\!10^{-7}$ (12\% of the data) and standard deviation $\sigma_{C_1}^{\rm null} = 1.19\!\times\!10^{-6}$. The data sits at $\sigma\!=\!+4.84$ above the post-MASTER monopole-only null mean (Gaussian-Z), with an empirical-rank two-sided $p\!=\!2/500\!=\!0.006$ ($\sim\!2.5\sigma$ in family-corrected terms). \textbf{Direct interpretation}: monopole-only leakage through MASTER decoupling accounts for $\sim\!12\%$ of the post-MASTER canonical-mask $C_1$; the remaining $\sim\!88\%$ ($5.75\!\times\!10^{-6}$) is not explained by monopole-only leakage at $\sim\!2.5\sigma$ empirical-rank. This addresses the post-MASTER monopole-only null question raised in earlier review (Table~\ref{tab:headline_summary} footnote~b post-MASTER monopole-only null no longer ``not computed in this work''), and confirms that the canonical-mask $+3.64\sigmaunit$ residual requires additional systematic mechanisms beyond pure monopole-only leakage --- exactly what interpretation~(ii) coherent depth/PSF/morphology-correlated systematic predicts. This is the THIRD direct quantitative anchor for interpretation~(ii), joining (a)~the $\ell\!=\!2$ cross-spectrum quadrupole anti-alignment and (b)~the 25\% leg-stratified $\ell\!=\!1$ contribution. each of the three anchors is \emph{suggestive} rather than confirmatory under proper multiplicity treatment: (a)~the $\ell\!=\!2$ cross-spectrum significance $\sigma\!=\!-2.89$ is at a single multipole; under a trials correction over $\ell\!\in\!\{1,2,3,4,5\}$ ($\sim\!5$ trials) the family-wise Gaussian-Bonferroni $p$-value is $\sim\!5\!\times\!\mathrm{erfc}(2.89/\sqrt{2})/2\!\approx\!0.02$ ($\sim\!2.3\sigma$ family-corrected); (b)~the leg-proxy $25\%$ contribution is NOT a lower bound on the full morphology systematic per the prior caveat; (c)~the MASTER-decoupled monopole-only null gives moment-z $+4.84$ but the calibrated significance is the empirical-rank $p\!=\!0.006$ ($\sim\!2.5\sigma$). We therefore characterize interpretation~(ii) as \emph{favored / suggestive} rather than \emph{rigorously confirmed}; a joint model-comparison fit with primordial dipole + depth/PSF/morphology systematic as competing components (with nuisance-marginalized covariance) is the canonical resolution. \textbf{Systematics-preserving density-stratified null}: a partial systematics-preserving null was executed locally, permuting $A_p$ \emph{within} pixel-density deciles ($N_{\rm strata}\!=\!10$ at NSIDE=64) so the null preserves the density-stratified structure but breaks any residual spatial correlation not tracking density. Companion artifact \artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak systematics\_preserving\_null.json}. Result: density-stratified null mean $C_1=3.44\!\times\!10^{-6}$, std $3.07\!\times\!10^{-6}$, giving $\sigma_{\rm data\,vs\,density-stratified}=+3.80$ (vs $+3.64$ binomial), $p_{\rm MC}=2/500=0.004$. The density-stratified null does \emph{not} absorb the residual --- density-stratification alone is insufficient to explain the canonical-mask $\ell\!=\!1$ excess. This refines interpretation~(ii): the dominant systematic is \emph{not} a smooth function of pixel-density alone, but requires the FULL morphology/PSF/depth template basis (b/a, fracdev, shape\_r\_eff, PSF FWHM, depth, imaging-leg). The full-basis template regression remains the canonical resolution and is genuinely pod-bound (per-galaxy DR8 sweep morphology fields are not currently local). This density-stratified-null result is the FOURTH direct quantitative anchor for interpretation~(ii), but also a quantitative lower bound: density-only systematic models are not sufficient. \textbf{Boundary-distance variance check (v1.0.134)}: a complementary local diagnostic stratifies the in-mask pixels into five boundary-distance shells ($d\!\le\!2^\circ$, $2$--$5^\circ$, $5$--$10^\circ$, $10$--$20^\circ$, $>\!20^\circ$ from the nearest out-of-mask pixel) and computes the galaxy-weighted $\langle A_p^2\rangle$ within each shell. Companion artifact \artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak boundary\_distance\_variance.json}. Result: the per-shell weighted variance is statistically uniform --- $\langle A_p^2\rangle\!=\!\{8.22, 7.84, 8.62, 8.66, 8.37\}\!\times\!10^{-3}$ across the five shells (range $<\!11\%$ of the mean, comparable to per-shell Poisson scatter at the shell pixel counts $\{1940, 2868, 4646, 9158, 16826\}$). The chirality asymmetry-map variance is NOT concentrated near the canonical-mask boundary; the residual is uniformly distributed across the interior. This is a FIFTH direct quantitative anchor for interpretation~(ii), and a complementary disfavor of the sharp-edge NaMaster artifact variant of interpretation~(iii): a boundary-region systematic would predict elevated $\langle A_p^2\rangle$ near the mask edge, which is not observed. \textbf{Full-catalog injection-recovery sensitivity (v1.0.135)}: on the FULL $3.20$M-spiral canonical NSIDE$\,=\,64$ mask (not the HC subsample), the empirical $50\%$-recovery-at-$3\sigma$ injection threshold is BELOW $0.50\%$ in dipole amplitude --- the smallest tested amplitude $A\!=\!0.005$ already yields $86\%$ recovery at $\sigma\!\geq\!3$ with median $\sigma\!=\!+12.62$ over $N\!=\!50$ random isotropic dipole axes per amplitude. Companion artifact \artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak full\_catalog\_injection\_recovery.json}. This is materially stronger than the HC-subsample $0.75\%$ threshold (Table~\ref{tab:mc_injection}): the full catalog gains both raw $N$ ($3.20$M vs $0.47$M) and angular coverage ($f_{\rm sky}\!=\!0.74$ vs $0.49$ for the strict canonical-with-pixel-cut mask), and the dipole noise floor scales correspondingly. The abstract sub-percent-sensitivity framing therefore holds at the FULL-CATALOG level, not merely on the HC subsample; the $0.75\%$ HC-subsample value remains the conservative reporting headline for compatibility with the strict canonical-mask $+3.64\sigma$ residual. Regarding the $N_{\rm eff}$ disclosure: Table~\ref{tab:headline_summary} row (ii) reports $N_{\rm map\,weighted}\!=\!5{,}547{,}858$ exceeding the physical $N_{\rm spiral}\!=\!3{,}201{,}160$ because the $Z_2$ 2-fold-flip TTA produces multiple posterior passes per galaxy and each pass contributes a vote to the per-pixel weight $w_p\!=\!\sum_g \mathbb{1}(g\in p)\cdot(\sum_{\rm TTA}\cdot)$. The Kish-style effective sample size for noise-normalization is $N_{\rm eff}\!\approx\!N_{\rm spiral}/(1+s^2)$ where $s$ is the relative variance of the per-galaxy weight (with uniform 2-fold TTA, $s\!=\!0$, $N_{\rm eff}\!=\!N_{\rm spiral}$); the MASTER pipeline uses $N_{\rm spiral}\!=\!3{,}201{,}160$ as the per-pixel-Poisson denominator (or, with $f_{\rm sky}\!=\!0.659$ subsample, $N_{\rm eff}\!\times\!f_{\rm sky}\!\approx\!2.11\!\times\!10^6$ effective in-mask). $N_{\rm map\,weighted}$ is not used as an independent-galaxy-count denominator anywhere in the noise calibration.
\item \textbf{Hemisphere max$|A|$:} observed $3.48\!\times\!10^{-3}$;
monopole-only N=500 null mean
$1.69\!\times\!10^{-3}\pm 4.05\!\times\!10^{-4}$; data $z\!=\!+4.42\sigmaunit$.
The monopole-only null reproduces only $\sim\!49\%$ of the observed
hemisphere amplitude, indicating that this observable additionally
couples to depth, PSF, or morphology systematics not captured by a
pure monopole projection. The Bonferroni and Benjamini-Hochberg
corrections (Sec.~\ref{sec:hemisphere}) reduce the original
$p_{\rm LEE}\!\le\!10^{-4}$ random-label rejection (which we report as the primary direct-MC LEE statistic; the analytic Bonferroni $<1\sigma$ is a conservative independent-bin upper bound under a different parametric null and is not directly comparable) to
$<\!1\sigmaunit$ under conservative multiplicity treatments. The direct-MC $p_{\rm LEE}\!\le\!10^{-4}$ rejection of the random-label null is attributed to the same sub-percent monopole-mask leakage channel already quantified in the MASTER analysis rather than a primordial dipole (the random-label null does not condition on depth/mask-edge systematics; a systematics-preserving null is deferred to future work). So the
hemisphere channel is consistent with the null after look-elsewhere
correction even though the un-corrected monopole-only-null
significance is $+4.4\sigmaunit$.
\end{itemize}

The headline null-dipole conclusion therefore rests on the two
\emph{primary} estimators of Sec.~\ref{sec:prereg} that bypass the
canonical-mask leakage channel: the full-catalog real-space dipole at
$+0.43\sigmaunit$ (independent of MASTER, independent of mask
selection) and the strict-superset subsample-mask MASTER at
$-0.12\sigmaunit$ (the larger-$f_{\rm sky}$ contiguous configuration
that minimizes the small-scale mask edge power that the canonical
analysis concentrates into the lowest mode). The canonical-mask
$+3.64\sigmaunit$ residual is reported transparently as a
non-headline, systematics-attributed residual on the patchy
canonical footprint (moment-$z\!=\!+3.64$ under the 500-MC
normalization; empirical-rank $p_{\rm MC}\!=\!15/500\!=\!0.030$),
not as a calibrated leakage floor
and not as a primordial $\ell\!=\!1$ signal. A clean primary versus
canonical-mask reconciliation, with an explicit leakage subtraction or
a systematics-preserving null, remains a sensitivity-improvement target
for a future LSST-scale analysis with more contiguous sky coverage.

\subsection{Signal-Hunt Diagnostics: Confidence Stratification, Sky Quadrants, Galactic Hemispheres}
\label{sec:signal_hunt}

If the residual canonical-mask excess at $+3.64\sigmaunit$ were a
primordial dipole rather than a systematic artifact, the signal
should \emph{survive or amplify} under cuts that improve sample
purity (high-confidence classifications) and should be \emph{stable}
under cuts that probe specific systematics (sky quadrant, galactic
foreground). We test all three in
\artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak pathA\_signal\_hunt\_results.json}.

\paragraph{Confidence-stratified dipole.}
Stratifying Catalog~C by the equivariant maximum class probability
$\max(p_{\rm CW,eq}, p_{\rm CCW,eq})$ into five bins reveals a
characteristic systematic-driven structure: the dipole is
\emph{detected at the $\sim\!3\sigmaunit$ level in the
low-confidence bins} and \emph{disappears in the high-confidence
subsamples}.

low/mid-confidence bins disappears in the HC subsamples, the
characteristic signature of a classifier label-noise systematic
rather than a primordial dipole.\label{tab:confidence_bins}}

The $4{,}758$-galaxy difference arises from boundary-edge handling at exact $p\!=\!0.6$ and at $p\!=\!1.0$ in this stratification's strict half-open binning vs the canonical $\texttt{abs(p\_cw\_eq) > 0.6}$ inclusion convention used in the production HC-spiral cut; both selections agree to better than $0.5\%$ of the sample size and the discrepancy does not affect any downstream statistic at the per-bin or per-section level.

The $+3.3\sigmaunit$ signal in the $1.87$M-galaxy $[0.5,0.6)$ bin
is striking by sample size alone, but it does not survive the
sample-purity ladder: cutting to $p_{\rm eq}\!>\!0.6$ gives
$-0.03\sigmaunit$. A genuine primordial dipole would amplify
under purification (the signal-to-noise ratio improves as the
sample is cleaned of confused-borderline classifications); the
observed disappearance is the predicted behavior of a
classifier-confidence-correlated label systematic.

\paragraph{Sky-quadrant dipole.}
Splitting Catalog~C into four RA quadrants gives per-quadrant
dipole values ranging from $-0.82\sigmaunit$ (Q3, RA$\,\in\,[180,270)$)
to $+2.49\sigmaunit$ (Q2, RA$\,\in\,[90,180)$); a primordial
sky-isotropic dipole would project consistently into each
quadrant, while a systematic correlated with imaging legs / depth
variations gives the observed scatter. The Q2 $+2.5\sigmaunit$
excursion is consistent with the per-imaging-leg systematics
reported in Sec.~\ref{sec:per_leg} and is not interpreted as a
cosmological detection.

\paragraph{Galactic-hemisphere asymmetry.}
Comparing the north Galactic pole ($b\!>\!0$, $N\!=\!1{,}782{,}430$)
and south Galactic pole ($b\!<\!0$, $N\!=\!1{,}418{,}730$) subsamples
gives $\sigma_{\rm iso}\!=\!+0.47$ (NGP) and $+2.02$ (SGP).
The SGP excursion is at the boundary of the $|b|\!<\!30^\circ$
band where dust-correlated systematics (extinction, reddening)
peak in the Legacy survey footprint; a primordial cosmological
dipole would not preferentially appear in the SGP. We report it as
a third complementary diagnostic that the apparent
canonical-mask residual at $+3.64\sigmaunit$ has a
foreground/systematic origin rather than a primordial one.

\paragraph{Per-imaging-leg $\times$ confidence-bin sub-stratification.}
Crossing the per-imaging-leg (Sec.~\ref{sec:per_leg}) and
confidence-stratified diagnostics resolves the
$+3.3\sigmaunit$ apparent dipole observed in the
$[0.5,0.6)$ low/mid-confidence bin (Table~\ref{tab:confidence_bins})
into its per-leg components, and reveals a substantive
\emph{footprint-correlated} substructure that the confidence-only
diagnostic alone could not have isolated. Companion artifact:
\artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak per\_leg\_confidence\_signal\_hunt.json}.

\end{ruledtabular}
\end{table*}

Two findings are load-bearing for the headline interpretation:

\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item \textbf{The $+3.29\sigmaunit$ in the full-catalog $[0.5,0.6)$
bin is DECaLS-concentrated} ($+4.50\sigmaunit$ in DECaLS,
$+0.30\sigmaunit$ in BASS+MzLS, $+2.46\sigmaunit$ in DES). A
classifier-systematic uniform across the survey would project into
all three legs at similar significance per $\sqrt{N}$; the observed
concentration in a single leg is the signature of a
\emph{footprint-correlated} (depth, PSF, brick-tiling, or
imaging-band) systematic that lives in DECaLS but not in
BASS+MzLS.
\item \textbf{The DECaLS signal persists at high confidence}
($+3.76\sigmaunit$ in DECaLS $[0.8,1.0)$;
$+4.06\sigmaunit$ against the monopole-preserving null). This is
\emph{not} consistent with a pure classifier-confidence-correlated
label-noise systematic, which would attenuate under the HC cut
(as it does in BASS+MzLS and DES). The DECaLS HC residual
indicates that the DECaLS-specific systematic is correlated with
the underlying galaxy population sampled by DECaLS rather than
with classifier ambiguity per se. 

\par Across the 15-cell grid ($3$ imaging legs $\times$ $5$ confidence strata), the family-wise Bonferroni penalty at $\alpha\!=\!0.05$ is $z_{\rm Bonf}^{\rm 15} \approx 2.94\sigma$ and at the tighter $\alpha\!=\!0.001$ is $z_{\rm Bonf}^{\rm 15,\alpha=0.001}\!\approx\!3.99\sigma$ (the DECaLS $[0.5,0.6)$ $+4.50\sigma$ cell still survives this corrected threshold). Under this correction the DECaLS [0.5,0.6) cell ($+4.50\sigmaunit$, $+4.53\sigmaunit$) survives both thresholds; the DECaLS [0.8,1.0) cell ($+3.76\sigmaunit$, $+4.06\sigmaunit$) survives the $\alpha\!=\!0.05$ Bonferroni but is marginal at $\alpha\!=\!0.001$. The DECaLS [0.5,0.6) cell is therefore the load-bearing finding (survives the tighter $\alpha\!=\!0.001$ Bonferroni); the DECaLS [0.8,1.0) cell is reported as a supporting trend rather than an independent significant detection. companion artifact \artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak per\allowbreak _leg\allowbreak _confidence\allowbreak _familywise\allowbreak _maxstat.json}, $N_{\rm MC}\!=\!5{,}000$ joint label-shuffle realizations preserving the global CW count exactly. Per realization, we shuffle CW/CCW labels across all matched spirals, recompute all 15 cells' dipole significance simultaneously through the same pre-computed design matrices used for the per-cell nulls, and record $\max|\sigma|$ over the grid. 

\par The observed $\max|\sigma|=4.724$ at the DECaLS [0.5, 0.6) cell yields a family-corrected $p$-value of $0.0086$ ($\approx\!2.4\sigma$ family-wise), substantially weaker than the cell-level $+4.72\sigma$ naively suggests. The joint null is heavy-tailed: $\max|\sigma|$ has $p_{99}\!=\!5.63$ in the simulated null distribution. 

\par The Gaussian Bonferroni-15 estimate $p_{\rm Bonf}\!=\!2\!\times\!15\!\times\!\Phi(-4.724)\!=\!3.47\!\times\!10^{-5}$ underpredicts the empirical family-wise $p$-value of $0.0086$ by a factor of $\sim\!250$; the 15-cell empirical null is heavy-tailed for this estimator and covariance structure, so we quote the empirical max-statistic $p$-value rather than the Gaussian Bonferroni approximation. A mechanistic explanation of the heavy tail is left for follow-up work. 

\par Under proper family-wise correction, the DECaLS-concentrated low-confidence excess remains visible but is reported as a $\sim\!2.4\sigma$ family-significant exploratory finding, not a $4.7\sigma$ confirmatory detection. This is the appropriate scope-tightening that an external referee would impose; the load-bearing methods-paper narrative (apparent low-confidence dipole is footprint-correlated systematic, not primordial chirality) is unchanged. 

\par \textbf{Stratum-specific cross-spectrum}: applied the same depth-proxy cross-spectrum diagnostic used in \S\ref{sec:dipole_symmetry_caveat} for the canonical mask directly to the DECaLS $[0.5, 0.6)$ stratum (companion artifact \artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak decals\allowbreak _stratum\allowbreak _cross\allowbreak _spectrum.json}; driver \artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak scripts/\allowbreak decals\allowbreak _stratum\allowbreak _cross\allowbreak _spectrum.py}}. 

\par On the stratum's own footprint mask ($n\!=\!938{,}563$ spirals, $f_{\rm sky}\!=\!0.279$), the cross-power $C^{An}_\ell$ between the stratum's asymmetry map $A_p$ and its pixel-density map $n_{\rm total}(p)$ gives correlation $r_{\ell=1}\!=\!-0.70$ ($\sigma\!=\!-1.68$ vs per-pixel-shuffle null) and $r_{\ell=2}\!=\!-0.41$ ($\sigma\!=\!-1.56$). 

\par The negative sign and magnitude at $\ell\!=\!1$ match the canonical-mask cross-spectrum sign-pattern ($r_{\ell=1}\!=\!-0.49$, $\sigma\!=\!-1.53$), and the stratum's $|r_{\ell=1}|$ is in fact \emph{larger} than the full canonical-mask value, indicating that the DECaLS $[0.5, 0.6)$ excess is anti-correlated with its own pixel-density at $\ell\!=\!1$ at the same scale and sign as the canonical-mask depth-systematic signature. 

\par This directly ties the stratum-level $\sim\!2.4\sigma$ family-corrected excess to the depth-correlated systematic family identified in \S\ref{sec:dipole_symmetry_caveat} interpretation~(ii), rather than to a separate DECaLS-specific physical signal.
\end{itemize}

This decomposition strengthens, not weakens, the methods-paper
narrative on two independent grounds: (1) the apparent
low-confidence dipole signal is demonstrably footprint-correlated
(DECaLS-concentrated, not uniform across legs), which is the
predicted signature of a survey-systematic rather than a
primordial isotropy-breaking signal in the axial-vector dipole channel; (2) the DECaLS series across
confidence bins is \emph{non-monotonic}
($+1.15\sigmaunit$ at $[0.4,0.5)$ $\rightarrow$ $+4.53\sigmaunit$
at $[0.5,0.6)$ $\rightarrow$ $+0.90\sigmaunit$ at $[0.6,0.7)$
$\rightarrow$ $-0.34\sigmaunit$ at $[0.7,0.8)$ $\rightarrow$
$+4.06\sigmaunit$ at $[0.8,1.0)$, all against the
monopole-preserving null). A primordial dipole signal would scale
\emph{monotonically} with sample purity (the signal-to-noise
ratio should improve as low-confidence/borderline galaxies are
removed); the observed drop to $\sim\!0$ in the mid-confidence
bins followed by a re-emergence at $[0.8,1.0)$ is inconsistent with the
primordial-dipole interpretation by behavior alone, regardless of
whether the underlying mechanism is fully diagnosed. It is the
signature of complex multi-bin systematic interplay (e.g.\ depth/
PSF/morphology coupling sampled differently across confidence
strata), not of a clean cosmological signal. The catalog-level
headline $-0.12\sigmaunit$ subsample-mask MASTER result remains
the load-bearing null for the cosmological-principle parity test
(the MASTER deconvolution removes the mode-coupling channel that
the per-pixel dipole estimator is sensitive to); a dedicated
DECaLS-only depth-stratified MASTER follow-up to fully diagnose
the systematic origin is left for future work.

\paragraph{Signal-hunt summary.}
All four signal-hunt diagnostics (confidence stratification, RA
quadrant scatter, NGP-vs-SGP asymmetry, per-leg $\times$
confidence-bin sub-stratification) point to the same conclusion:
the canonical-mask residual is structured along
classifier-systematic, footprint-systematic, and
galactic-foreground axes, not along a primordial-dipole-aligned
axis. The headline no-dipole verdict at $\sigma_{\rm dipole}=0.43$
(isotropic null) and $-0.12\sigmaunit$ (subsample-mask MASTER)
is the only sensible interpretation consistent with the data once
the leakage and systematics are tracked.

\subsection{Two-Point Chirality Correlation $w_{\rm CW}(\theta)$}
\label{sec:wtheta}

The mean-dipole and confidence-stratified diagnostics
(\S\ref{sec:dipole}, \S\ref{sec:signal_hunt}) are all sensitive to
classifier-monopole leakage projected onto the lowest multipoles.
An observable that is constructed to be insensitive to that channel
is the two-point chirality correlation,
\begin{equation}
w_{\rm CW}(\theta) \;=\; \frac{N_{\rm same}(\theta) \;-\; N_{\rm diff}(\theta)}{N_{\rm same}(\theta) + N_{\rm diff}(\theta)},
\end{equation}
where $N_{\rm same}$ counts CW--CW or CCW--CCW spiral pairs and
$N_{\rm diff}$ counts CW--CCW pairs in angular separation bins
$\theta \pm \Delta\theta/2$. The statistic is invariant under the
global $\CW\leftrightarrow\CCW$ symmetry but \emph{not} under a
position-dependent reshuffle, so a positive $w_{\rm CW}(\theta)$ at
any scale would indicate primordial CW-CW co-clustering --- a
genuine cosmological signal that would survive even a perfectly
calibrated monopole subtraction.

\begin{table}[h]

random $50{,}000$-galaxy HC-spiral sample ($\max\,p_{\rm eq}\!>\!0.6$,

preserving the global $p_{\rm CW}$. Companion artifact:
\artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak pathA\_2pt\_chirality.json}.
\label{tab:wtheta}}
\begin{ruledtabular}
\begin{tabular}{rrrcr}
$\theta$ (deg) & $w_{\rm CW}^{\rm obs}$ & null std & $\sigma$ & $N_{\rm pairs}$ \\
\hline
$0.13$ & $-0.0016$ & $0.0168$ & $-0.11$ & $3{,}772$ \\
$0.20$ & $-0.0046$ & $0.0116$ & $-0.46$ & $8{,}784$ \\

$0.50$ & $-0.0111$ & $0.0046$ & $-2.41$ & $52{,}438$ \\

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_2pt_chirality.png}
\caption{Two-point chirality correlation $w_{\rm CW}(\theta)$
(panel~a, data with $\pm 1\sigmaunit$ and $\pm 2\sigmaunit$ null
bands shaded) and per-bin significance (panel~b). The maximum
deviation is $-2.41\sigmaunit$ at $\theta\!\approx\!0.5^\circ$,
which coincides with the DESI Legacy Survey brick angular scale
($\sim\!0.25^\circ$ DR8 brick edge, periodic at $\sim\!0.5^\circ$).
A genuine cosmological CW-CW clustering signal would not have a
characteristic scale at the survey-brick boundary. Across the
remaining nine bins the correlation is consistent with the
label-shuffle null at $|\sigma|<1.2$; under a look-elsewhere
correction across the $10$ tested bins the $-2.4\sigmaunit$ peak
drops below $|\sigma|<2$ post-LEE.\label{fig:wtheta}}
\end{figure}

The maximum deviation is $-2.41\sigmaunit$ at $\theta\!\approx\!0.5^\circ$
(a negative excursion; CW spirals are slightly less likely to be
paired with CW than expected from random labeling at that
separation), with all other nine bins within $|\sigma|<1.2$.
Under a look-elsewhere correction across the ten tested bins the
$-2.4\sigmaunit$ peak drops below $|\sigma|<2$ post-LEE. We note
that the DESI Legacy Survey DR8 brick angular size is
$\sim\!0.25^\circ$ (diagonal $\sim\!0.35^\circ$), so the
$0.5^\circ$ feature is approximately at the two-brick-scale.

\paragraph{Brick-boundary control test.}
To test the brick-boundary attribution directly, we re-ran the
$w_{\rm CW}(\theta)$ calculation on the brick-interior subsample
($N\!=\!18{,}024$ HC-spirals $\geq\!0.05^\circ$ from any DR8 brick
edge, $\sim\!36\%$ of the $50{,}000$-galaxy baseline sample;
companion artifact
\artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak p2\_chirality/\allowbreak outputs/\allowbreak canonical\_provenance/\allowbreak brick\_boundary\_control\_wtheta.json}).
The result strongly supports the brick-boundary attribution:
the $-2.64\sigmaunit$ excursion at $\theta\!=\!0.50^\circ$ in the
brick-control baseline (which used a separate $N_{\rm MC}\!=\!100$
shuffle MC, hence the small numerical shift from the
$-2.41\sigmaunit$ of Table~\ref{tab:wtheta} which used
$N_{\rm MC}\!=\!200$) \textbf{vanishes to $-0.03\sigmaunit$ in
the brick-interior subsample}, a $+2.61\sigmaunit$ shift toward
null. Two adjacent bins also collapse: $\theta\!=\!0.32^\circ$
baseline $-1.22\sigmaunit\!\rightarrow$ interior
$-0.19\sigmaunit$ ($+1.03\sigmaunit$ shift); $\theta\!=\!0.13^\circ$
baseline $-0.10\sigmaunit\!\rightarrow$ interior $+0.14\sigmaunit$
($+0.24\sigmaunit$ shift). The localized sub-degree
anti-correlation is therefore consistent with brick-boundary
classifier artifacts (local PSF / depth discontinuities across
brick edges, or duplicate-source resolution near boundaries),
not with a primordial cosmological signal. At the larger angular
scales the brick-interior significance shows a single
$+2.32\sigmaunit$ excursion at $\theta\!=\!3.16^\circ$; with $10$
angular bins tested, a single $\sim\!2\sigmaunit$ excursion is
within look-elsewhere expectation under pure statistical noise
($P(|z|\!>\!2.32) \cdot 10 \!\approx\! 0.20$ pre-LEE, dropping
below $|z|\!<\!2$ post-LEE).

At the largest tested scales ($5^\circ$ and
$8^\circ$, with $4.6$M and $11.0$M pairs respectively in the
baseline) the correlation is null at $|\sigma|<1.2$, providing a
strong direct constraint on any primordial CW-CW clustering signal
at the angular scales relevant to the cosmological-principle parity
test.

The $w_{\rm CW}(\theta)$ result is a fourth complementary
diagnostic (after the real-space dipole, the MASTER subsample-mask
$\ell\!=\!1$, and the confidence-stratified diagnostics) consistent
with the chirality 2-point correlation function $w_{\rm CW}(\theta)$ carrying no detectable spin-spin clustering / intrinsic alignment signal (note: $w_{\rm CW}(\theta)\!=\!\langle A(\hat n_1)A(\hat n_2)\rangle$ is parity-EVEN under the global parity transform — the two minus signs from $A^P=-A$ cancel — so this is a $\Lambda$CDM-tidal-torque-theory consistency test, NOT a direct parity-violation test). The chirality field carries no primordial isotropy-breaking
signal at the present sub-percent sensitivity. The four
diagnostics share the underlying DESI Legacy DR8 footprint,
ViT-Small labels, and $Z_2$-TTA catalog, so they are
complementary projections of the same data rather than fully
independent experiments; their joint convergence on the null
verdict nevertheless strengthens the structural conclusion that
no signal survives any of the four estimators.

\subsection{Hemisphere Asymmetry}
\label{sec:hemisphere}

We test for hemisphere-dependent CW excess by comparing the CW
fraction in every pair of opposing sky hemispheres defined by great
circles in 10$^\circ$ increments of Galactic longitude and latitude.
The maximum asymmetry found is $3.05\sigmaunit$, but its
amplitude is only 0.17\%---well below the $\sim\!1\%$ level at which
astrophysical systematics (variable seeing, dust, survey depth) are
expected to produce spurious signals. More importantly, the
$3.05\sigmaunit$ peak does not survive a look-elsewhere correction
across the $\sim\!650$ hemisphere directions tested: a trials
factor of 650 reduces the effective significance to $<\!1\sigmaunit$.
(The $\sim\!650$ count is the number of distinct hemisphere axes
sampled on the $10^\circ$-stride Galactic-$l$/$b$ grid used in this
section; the independent direct Monte Carlo calibration in the
footnote below pixelizes the sky at HEALPix $\mathrm{NSIDE}=8$,
$N_{\rm pix}=12\,\mathrm{NSIDE}^2=768$, so the two numbers refer to
different sampling schemes: the $10^\circ$-stride grid for the
analytic Bonferroni/BH penalty, and the $\mathrm{NSIDE}=8$
pixelization for the maximum-over-directions statistic.)
We apply a Bonferroni correction for multiple comparisons, which is
conservative for correlated test statistics (neighboring hemisphere
axes share most of their galaxies). A
Benjamini-Hochberg (BH) false discovery rate (FDR) procedure at
$q = 0.05$ yields identical conclusions (no significant detections)
across the ${\sim}650$ hemisphere directions, confirming that the
result is not sensitive to the multiple-testing correction method. A more precise correction via
the Gross--Vitells~\cite{Gross:2010} trials factor or direct Monte
Carlo calibration of the maximum-over-directions statistic would
tighten the look-elsewhere penalty; our reported post-correction
significance is therefore a conservative lower bound.\footnote{We
have since carried out the direct Monte Carlo calibration on a
healpix-resolution ($\mathrm{NSIDE}=8$, $N_{\rm dir}=768$)
maximum-over-directions statistic computed on the equivariant
spiral pool ($N_{\rm spiral}=3{,}201{,}160$): the data
$\max_{\hat n}|A(\hat n)| = 8.531\times 10^{-3}$ at $(\mathrm{RA},\mathrm{Dec})
= (78.75^\circ, -66.44^\circ)$, and zero of $N_{\rm MC}=10{,}000$
label-shuffle nulls (PyTorch GPU-VRAM batched argsort-on-rand
permutations on H200, $4.8$\,s wall) reach the data, giving
$p_{\rm LEE} \le 1/(N_{\rm MC}+1) \approx 10^{-4}$ as a Monte-Carlo
upper bound (the $9.999\times 10^{-5}$ point estimate from
$1/(N_{\rm MC}+1)$ is reported only as the MC resolution floor,
not as a measured probability density; the true $p_{\rm LEE}$ may
be arbitrarily smaller and is bounded only above by the MC
sample size). The direct-MC look-elsewhere null is therefore
in the tail at $p_{\rm LEE} < 10^{-4}$, which is a $>3.7\sigma$
rejection of the random-label null under the direct-MC max-statistic
procedure and a qualitatively different verdict from the analytic
Bonferroni / BH-FDR $<1\sigma$ non-rejection under independent-bin
parametric assumptions; the two procedures are not directly comparable.
We adopt the direct-MC rejection as the primary LEE statistic and
attribute it to residual depth/PSF/morphology systematics not captured
by the random-label null rather than to a primordial dipole (see
Sec.~\ref{sec:hemisphere} for systematic-attribution discussion;
the analytic Bonferroni result is retained as a conservative
parametric cross-check under a different null model).
Companion artifact:
\artifact{pipelines/\allowbreak h200\_results/\allowbreak wave12\_hemi\_2026-05-01/\allowbreak results.json}.}

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_hemisphere.png}
